P1-1.1-INTRO-01 · Intro · 1.1-LT · 1.1-INTRO-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC1 — Find matching values across different representations.
1.1-LT — Connect tables, graphs, equations or rules, and contexts that represent the same relationship.

Do the graph and table represent the same relationship? Support your answer with at least two matching or conflicting values.

xy
-1-1
13
37
Answer / solution
Answer: Yes

The displayed input-output values agree with points on the graph, so they show the same relationship.

P1-1.1-INTRO-02 · Intro · 1.1-LT · 1.1-INTRO-IC2 · REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01 · student_constructed_table
IC2 — Create a table or graph from another representation.
1.1-LT — Connect tables, graphs, equations or rules, and contexts that represent the same relationship.

Use the rule \(y=3x-2\) to complete the table.

xy
0 
1 
2 
3 
Answer / solution
Answer: (0, -2), (1, 1), (2, 4), (3, 7)

Substitute each x-value into the rule. The completed rows are (0, -2), (1, 1), (2, 4), (3, 7).

P1-1.1-INTRO-03 · Intro · 1.1-LT · 1.1-INTRO-IC3 · ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC3 — Check whether two representations describe the same relationship.
1.1-LT — Connect tables, graphs, equations or rules, and contexts that represent the same relationship.

Use the graph and table to find the rate of change shown by each representation. Explain whether the evidence matches.

xy
-16
12
3-2
Answer / solution
Answer: graph rate -2; table rate -2; they match

The graph has rate -2. The table also changes at rate -2, so they match.

P1-1.1-INTRO-04 · Intro · 1.1-LT · 1.1-INTRO-IC4 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC4 — Use values, rate, or pattern as evidence.
1.1-LT — Connect tables, graphs, equations or rules, and contexts that represent the same relationship.

Determine the rate of change from the graph and from the table. State whether the two rates agree and cite the change you used.

xy
-1-4
14
312
Answer / solution
Answer: graph rate 4; table rate 4; they match

The graph has rate 4. The table also changes at rate 4, so they match.

P1-1.1-INTRO-05 · Intro · 1.1-LT · 1.1-INTRO-IC1 · ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01 · mixed_table_equation
IC1 — Find matching values across different representations.
1.1-LT — Connect tables, graphs, equations or rules, and contexts that represent the same relationship.

Compare the table with the rule \(y=-3x+5\). Do they represent the same relationship? Use values or rate as evidence.

xy
-18
12
3-4
Answer / solution
Answer: Yes

Substitution gives the same outputs as the table and both have rate -3.

P1-1.1-INTRO-06 · Intro · 1.1-LT · 1.1-INTRO-IC2 · REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01 · student_constructed_coordinate_graph
IC2 — Create a table or graph from another representation.
1.1-LT — Connect tables, graphs, equations or rules, and contexts that represent the same relationship.

Graph the relationship from the table. Verify two plotted points by writing their coordinates.

xy
-1-1
13
37
Answer / solution
Answer: (-1, -1), (1, 3), (3, 7)

Plot each table row as (x,y). The points are (-1, -1), (1, 3), (3, 7).

P1-1.1-INTRO-07 · Intro · 1.1-LT · 1.1-INTRO-IC3 · CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · mixed_table_equation
IC3 — Check whether two representations describe the same relationship.
1.1-LT — Connect tables, graphs, equations or rules, and contexts that represent the same relationship.

Compare the table with the rule \(y=3x-2\). Do they represent the same relationship? Use values or rate as evidence.

xy
-1-5
11
37
Answer / solution
Answer: Yes

Substitution gives the same outputs as the table and both have rate 3.

P1-1.1-REVIEW-01 · Review · U2-MG04 · U2-MG04-IC1 · REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01 · student_constructed_coordinate_graph
IC1 — Make a graph from a table.
U2-MG04 — Create a new representation of a relationship and check that the important values and pattern stay the same.

Use the table to graph the relationship on the blank coordinate plane. Then write two coordinates you used to check your graph.

xy
-2-5
01
27
Answer / solution
Answer: (-2, -5), (0, 1), (2, 7)

Plot the three table rows as ordered pairs. Any two of (-2,-5), (0,1), and (2,7) can be used to verify the graph.

P1-1.1-REVIEW-04 · Review · U2-MG04 · U2-MG04-IC4 · ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC4 — Check matching inputs, outputs, and patterns to make sure the representations agree.
U2-MG04 — Create a new representation of a relationship and check that the important values and pattern stay the same.

Calculate the rate of change shown by the graph and by the table. Compare the two results and explain whether they agree.

xy
-1-5
11
39
Answer / solution
Answer: graph rate 3; table does not keep rate 3

The graph has rate 3. The last table value breaks that pattern, so the representations do not match.

P1-1.1-REVIEW-07 · Review · U2-MG04 · U2-MG04-IC3 · REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01 · context_to_equation
IC3 — Use a context to make a table, graph, or rule when enough information is given.
U2-MG04 — Create a new representation of a relationship and check that the important values and pattern stay the same.

A rental costs $7 to start and $4 per hour. Write an equation for total cost \(C\) after \(h\) hours.

Answer / solution
Answer: C=4h+7

The fixed starting cost is 7 and the hourly rate is 4, so C=4h+7.

P1-1.1-MASTERY-01 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-CONSTRUCT-OR-CRITIQUE-01 · student_constructed_coordinate_graph
IC1 — Plot and read ordered pairs.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Plot the points (-4, 2), (1, -4), (6, 3) on the coordinate plane. Then write the coordinates of the point with the smallest x-coordinate.

Answer / solution
Answer: (-4, 2)

The point with the smallest x-coordinate is (-4,2).

P1-1.1-MASTERY-02 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC2 · REPRESENTATION-CONSTRUCT-OR-CRITIQUE-01 · student_constructed_coordinate_graph
IC2 — Use the x- and y-axes correctly.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Plot the points (-6, 5), (2, -1), (-1, -6) on the coordinate plane. Then identify the plotted point farthest to the left.

Answer / solution
Answer: (-6, 5)

The leftmost plotted point has x=-6, so it is (-6,5).

P1-1.1-MASTERY-03 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC3 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC3 — Read a graph using its scale.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Use the graph and its scale to find the y-value when \(x=3\).

Answer / solution
Answer: 4

At x=3, the plotted line has y=4.

P1-1.1-MASTERY-04 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC4 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC4 — Identify and interpret intercepts when they are shown.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Use the graph to identify the y-intercept. Give its coordinate.

Answer / solution
Answer: (0, -3)

The y-intercept occurs where x=0. The graph crosses the y-axis at y=-3, so the intercept is (0, -3).

P1-1.1-MASTERY-05 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC1 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC1 — Plot and read ordered pairs.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Read the coordinates of points A and C from the graph.

Answer / solution
Answer: A=(-6, 4), C=(5, 3)

Read horizontal x then vertical y: A=(-6, 4) and C=(5, 3).

P1-1.1-MASTERY-06 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC2 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC2 — Use the x- and y-axes correctly.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Point B is shown on the graph. What is its x-coordinate, and which axis tells you that value?

Answer / solution
Answer: 3; the x-axis

Read the horizontal coordinate first. Point B has x=3, which is read from the x-axis.

P1-1.1-MASTERY-07 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC3 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC3 — Read a graph using its scale.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Read the coordinate graph. What output corresponds to \(x=2\)?

Answer / solution
Answer: -1

At x=2, the plotted line has y=-1.

P1-1.1-MASTERY-08 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC4 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_context_graph
IC4 — Identify and interpret intercepts when they are shown.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Read the point where the graph crosses the y-axis. State that intercept and interpret it as the amount at time 0.

Answer / solution
Answer: (0, 10); the amount starts at 10 units

At x=0 the graph is at y=10. The y-intercept is (0, 10), so the starting amount is 10 units.

P1-1.2-INTRO-01 · Intro · 1.2-LT · 1.2-INTRO-IC1 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · semantic_data_table
IC1 — Identify the input and output in a relationship.
1.2-LT — Use input-output rules and the basic idea of a function.

For the row with input 2, identify the input and the output.

inputoutput
-18
05
2-1
4-7
Answer / solution
Answer: input 2, output -1

Read across the row: input=2 and output=-1.

P1-1.2-INTRO-02 · Intro · 1.2-LT · 1.2-INTRO-IC2 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC2 — Use a rule to find outputs.
1.2-LT — Use input-output rules and the basic idea of a function.

Use the rule \(y=2x+1\) to find the outputs for inputs [-2, 1, 4].

Answer / solution
Answer: [-3, 3, 9]

Substitute each input into the rule. The outputs are [-3, 3, 9].

P1-1.2-INTRO-03 · Intro · 1.2-LT · 1.2-INTRO-IC3 · REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01 · semantic_data_table
IC3 — Write ordered pairs from inputs and outputs.
1.2-LT — Use input-output rules and the basic idea of a function.

Write the input-output rows as ordered pairs in \((input, output)\) order.

inputoutput
-1-5
0-2
24
410
Answer / solution
Answer: (-1, -5), (0, -2), (2, 4), (4, 10)

Write each row as (input, output): (-1, -5), (0, -2), (2, 4), (4, 10).

P1-1.2-INTRO-04 · Intro · 1.2-LT · 1.2-INTRO-IC4 · CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · semantic_data_table
IC4 — Decide whether each input has only one output.
1.2-LT — Use input-output rules and the basic idea of a function.

Is the relation in the table a function? Explain using the one-output-per-input rule.

inputoutput
14
26
27
48
Answer / solution
Answer: No, it is not a function.

Input 2 is paired with two different outputs, so it is not a function.

Complete metadata / map trace

P1-1.2-INTRO-05 · Intro · 1.2-LT · 1.2-INTRO-IC1 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_mapping_diagram
IC1 — Identify the input and output in a relationship.
1.2-LT — Use input-output rules and the basic idea of a function.

In the mapping, what output is connected to input 2?

  • 1→4
  • 2→5
  • 3→5
Answer / solution
Answer: 5

Follow the arrow from input 2; it points to output 5.

P1-1.2-INTRO-06 · Intro · 1.2-LT · 1.2-INTRO-IC2 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · semantic_data_table
IC2 — Use a rule to find outputs.
1.2-LT — Use input-output rules and the basic idea of a function.

Use the rule \(y=-3x+5\) to fill in the missing outputs.

inputoutput
-1 
0 
2 
4 
Answer / solution
Answer: 8, 5, -1, -7

Substitute each input into y=-3x+5. The outputs are 8, 5, -1, -7.

P1-1.2-INTRO-07 · Intro · 1.2-LT · 1.2-INTRO-IC3 · REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01 · supplied_mapping_diagram
IC3 — Write ordered pairs from inputs and outputs.
1.2-LT — Use input-output rules and the basic idea of a function.

Write the mapping as a set of ordered pairs in \((input, output)\) order.

  • 1→4
  • 2→5
  • 3→5
Answer / solution
Answer: [(1, 4), (2, 5), (3, 5)]

Follow each arrow from input to output. The ordered pairs are [(1, 4), (2, 5), (3, 5)].

P1-1.2-INTRO-08 · Intro · 1.2-LT · 1.2-INTRO-IC4 · CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · supplied_mapping_diagram
IC4 — Decide whether each input has only one output.
1.2-LT — Use input-output rules and the basic idea of a function.

Is the mapping a function? Explain using the one-output-per-input condition.

  • 1→4
  • 1→5
  • 3→5
Answer / solution
Answer: No

Input 1 points to two different outputs, so the relation is not a function.

P1-1.2-REVIEW-01 · Review · U2-MG03 · U2-MG03-IC1 · CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · semantic_data_table
IC1 — Decide whether a table is a function.
U2-MG03 — Use inputs and outputs to decide whether a relationship is a function.

Decide whether the table defines a function. Justify your decision by checking whether each input has only one output.

inputoutput
14
26
36
48
Answer / solution
Answer: Yes, it is a function.

Each input appears with only one output; repeated outputs are allowed.

P1-1.2-REVIEW-02 · Review · U2-MG03 · U2-MG03-IC2 · CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · equation_relationship
IC2 — Decide whether ordered pairs or a mapping are a function.
U2-MG03 — Use inputs and outputs to decide whether a relationship is a function.

Do the ordered pairs [(-2, 3), (-2, 6), (2, 7)] represent a function? Explain.

Answer / solution
Answer: No

A relation is a function only if every input has exactly one output. The input -2 has two different outputs.

P1-1.2-REVIEW-03 · Review · U2-MG03 · U2-MG03-IC3 · CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC3 — Use a graph to decide whether each input has only one output.
U2-MG03 — Use inputs and outputs to decide whether a relationship is a function.

Does the graph represent a function? Explain whether any input has more than one output.

Answer / solution
Answer: Yes

Each x-value on the line has one y-value, so it is a function.

P1-1.2-MASTERY-01 · Mastery · U1-MG03 · U1-MG03-IC1 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC1 — Substitute a value for a variable.
U1-MG03 — Evaluate numerical and algebraic expressions using substitution and order of operations.

Evaluate \(2x+4\) for \(x=2\).

Answer / solution
Answer: 8

Substitute the given value(s) with parentheses and follow order of operations. The result is 8.

P1-1.2-MASTERY-04 · Mastery · U1-MG03 · U1-MG03-IC4 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · equation_relationship
IC4 — Check whether an answer is reasonable.
U1-MG03 — Evaluate numerical and algebraic expressions using substitution and order of operations.

For \(x=-6\), a student claims \(5x+5=25\). Evaluate the expression and decide whether the claim is reasonable.

Answer / solution
Answer: -25; claim is not reasonable

Substitute x=-6: 5(-6)+5=-25. Because the product is negative with magnitude greater than 5, a positive result is not reasonable.

P1-1.2-MASTERY-06 · Mastery · U1-MG03 · U1-MG03-IC2 · ERROR-DIAGNOSE-WORKED-SOLUTION-01 · worked_expression
IC2 — Use order of operations correctly.
U1-MG03 — Evaluate numerical and algebraic expressions using substitution and order of operations.

A student wrote \(6-2(3+1)=4(4)=16\). Identify the first error and give the correct value of \(6-2(3+1)\).

Answer / solution
Answer: -2

The student subtracted 2 from 6 before completing multiplication. Evaluate parentheses first: 3+1=4, then 2·4=8, then 6-8=-2.

P1-1.2-MASTERY-08 · Mastery · U1-MG03 · U1-MG03-IC4 · ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01 · equation_relationship
IC4 — Check whether an answer is reasonable.
U1-MG03 — Evaluate numerical and algebraic expressions using substitution and order of operations.

For \(x=-5\), a student claims \(5x+6=19\). Evaluate the expression and decide whether the claim is reasonable.

Answer / solution
Answer: -19; claim is not reasonable

Substitute x=-5: 5(-5)+6=-19. Because the product is negative with magnitude greater than 6, a positive result is not reasonable.

P1-1.3-INTRO-02 · Intro · 1.3-LT · 1.3-INTRO-IC2 · CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · supplied_graph_grid
IC2 — Notice basic quadratic, exponential, and absolute-value shapes or patterns.
1.3-LT — Notice how common function families change in different ways.

Match graphs A-D to the families linear, quadratic, exponential, and absolute value. Use shape evidence, not position alone.

Answer / solution
Answer: A=quadratic, B=linear, C=absolute value, D=exponential

A has a parabola shape; B is a straight line; C has a V-shape; D has multiplicative curved growth. The panel order is intentionally not the family-list order.

P1-1.3-INTRO-04 · Intro · 1.3-LT · 1.3-INTRO-IC4 · ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC4 — Explain why one family is more likely than another.

A student claims both representations are linear. Evaluate the claim using evidence from the graph and table.

xy
00
11
24
39
Answer / solution
Answer: The claim is false.

The graph is a straight line with constant rate, but the table has changing first differences and constant second differences, so the table is quadratic.

P1-1.3-INTRO-08 · Intro · 1.3-LT · 1.3-INTRO-IC4 · CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · mixed_table_equation
IC4 — Explain why one family is more likely than another.
1.3-LT — Notice how common function families change in different ways.

Which is more likely for this relationship: linear or exponential? Use the table and equation \(y=3\cdot 2^x\) as evidence.

xy
03
16
212
324
Answer / solution
Answer: Exponential

The table doubles each step, and the equation multiplies by a constant base 2, so exponential fits better than linear.

P1-1.3-REVIEW-01 · Review · U2-MG01 · U2-MG01-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC1 — Compare two relationships shown in different representations.
U2-MG01 — Compare relationships across graphs, tables, equations, and contexts using clear evidence.

Relationship A is shown by the graph and Relationship B by the table. Compare the two relationships using a common feature.

xy (B)
01
211
421
Answer / solution
Answer: A rate=2, start=5; B rate=5, start=1; faster=B

Read A's rate/start from the graph and compute B's from table differences. A: 2, 5. B: 5, 1. Therefore B grows faster.

29
415
Answer / solution

P1-1.3-REVIEW-04 · Review · U2-MG01 · U2-MG01-IC4 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_table_equation
IC4 — Support a comparison with information from the representations.
U2-MG01 — Compare relationships across graphs, tables, equations, and contexts using clear evidence.

Make one defensible comparison between the table and the equation \(y=5x+2\), and support it with specific numerical evidence from both.

xy (A)
05
211
417
Answer / solution
Answer: A rate=3, start=5; B rate=5, start=2

From the table, A changes by 3 per 1 x and starts at 5. In the equation, B has coefficient 5 and constant 2. These values support the comparison.

P1-1.3-REVIEW-05 · Review · U2-MG01 · U2-MG01-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_table_equation
IC1 — Compare two relationships shown in different representations.
U2-MG01 — Compare relationships across graphs, tables, equations, and contexts using clear evidence.

Compare Relationship A in the table with Relationship B, \(y=3x+1\), using the same mathematical feature for both.

xy (A)
03
27
411
Answer / solution
Answer: A rate=2, start=3; B rate=3, start=1

From the table, A changes by 2 per 1 x and starts at 3. In the equation, B has coefficient 3 and constant 1. These values support the comparison.

P1-1.3-REVIEW-06 · Review · U2-MG01 · U2-MG01-IC2 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC2 — Use values, rates, starting values, or patterns as evidence.
U2-MG01 — Compare relationships across graphs, tables, equations, and contexts using clear evidence.

Use the graph and table to extract a rate and a starting value for each relationship. What evidence is most useful for comparing them?

xy (B)
02
210
418
Answer / solution
Answer: A rate=3, start=4; B rate=4, start=2; faster=B

Read A's rate/start from the graph and compute B's from table differences. A: 3, 4. B: 4, 2. Therefore B grows faster.

xy (A)
05
29
413
Answer / solution

From the table, A changes by 2 per 1 x and starts at 5. In the equation, B has coefficient 5 and constant 1. These values support the comparison.

Write a concise comparison claim about Relationships A and B and support it with specific evidence from both the graph and table.

xy (B)
02
28
414
Answer / solution
Answer: A rate=3, start=3; B rate=3, start=2; faster=same rate

Both rates are 3; compare starts 3 and 2.

Mastery · 8 questions

P1-1.3-MASTERY-01 · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC1 — Find slope from a graph.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

Use two points on the graph to find the slope. Show \(\frac{\Delta y}{\Delta x}\).

Answer / solution
Answer: 2

For example, use (-2,-3) and (2,5). Slope=(5--3)/(2--2)=2.

P1-1.3-MASTERY-02 · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC2 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · semantic_data_table
IC2 — Find slope from a table.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

Use the table to calculate the rate of change.

xy
0-2
24
410
Answer / solution
Answer: 3

Choose two rows and compute change in y divided by change in x. The constant rate is 3.

P1-1.3-MASTERY-03 · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC3 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC3 — Find slope from an equation.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

What is the slope of \(y=-2x+5\)?

Answer / solution
Answer: -2

In y=mx+b, the coefficient of x is the slope. Here m=-2.

P1-1.3-MASTERY-04 · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC4 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · context_quantitative
IC4 — Find and interpret rate of change from a context.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

A machine produces \(16\) parts by hour \(2\) and \(32\) parts by hour \(6\), at a constant rate. Find and interpret the rate.

Answer / solution
Answer: 4 parts/hour

Rate=(32-16)/(6-2)=4. Production increases by 4 parts for each additional hour.

P1-1.3-MASTERY-06 · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC2 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · semantic_data_table
IC2 — Find slope from a table.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

Use two rows of the table to compute the change in y divided by the change in x and state the constant rate.

xy
-1-5
11
410
Answer / solution
Answer: 3

Choose two rows and compute change in y divided by change in x. The constant rate is 3.

P1-1.3-MASTERY-07 · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC3 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC3 — Find slope from an equation.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

Find the slope of \(2y=-4x+10\).

Answer / solution
Answer: -2

Divide both sides by 2: y=-2x+5. The slope is -2.

P1-1.3-MASTERY-08 · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC4 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · context_quantitative
IC4 — Find and interpret rate of change from a context.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

A tank has \(18\) liters after \(2\) minutes and \(30\) liters after \(6\) minutes, changing at a constant rate. Find the rate and predict the amount after \(8\) minutes.

Answer / solution
Answer: 3 L/min; 36 L

The amount changes by 12 liters over 4 minutes, so the rate is 3 L/min. Two more minutes adds 6 liters, giving 36 L at 8 minutes.

Section 1.4 · Practice 1

P1-1.4-INTRO-01 · Intro · 1.4-LT · 1.4-INTRO-IC1 · CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · equation_relationship
IC1 — Identify terms, coefficients, and factors.
1.4-LT — Read, evaluate, and rewrite expressions without changing their value.

In \(5x-3y+8\), identify the coefficient of \(x\) and list the three terms.

Answer / solution
Answer: coefficient 5; terms 5x, -3y, 8

A coefficient is the numerical factor on a variable term. Terms are separated by addition/subtraction signs.

P1-1.4-INTRO-02 · Intro · 1.4-LT · 1.4-INTRO-IC2 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC2 — Substitute values into an expression.
1.4-LT — Read, evaluate, and rewrite expressions without changing their value.

Evaluate \(3x-2y+5\) for \(x=-3\) and \(y=4\).

Answer / solution
Answer: -12

Substitute with parentheses: 3(-3)-2(4)+5=-12.

P1-1.4-INTRO-03 · Intro · 1.4-LT · 1.4-INTRO-IC3 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC3 — Combine like terms.
1.4-LT — Read, evaluate, and rewrite expressions without changing their value.

Simplify \(4x+7-3x-2\).

Answer / solution
Answer: 1x+5

Combine x-terms: 4+(-3)=1. Combine constants: 7-2=5.

P1-1.4-INTRO-04 · Intro · 1.4-LT · 1.4-INTRO-IC4 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC4 — Use the distributive property correctly.
1.4-LT — Read, evaluate, and rewrite expressions without changing their value.

Use the distributive property to rewrite \(5(4x-3)\).

Answer / solution
Answer: 20x-15

Multiply 5 by each term inside the parentheses, giving 20x-15.

P1-1.4-INTRO-05 · Intro · 1.4-LT · 1.4-INTRO-IC1 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · equation_relationship
IC1 — Identify terms, coefficients, and factors.
1.4-LT — Read, evaluate, and rewrite expressions without changing their value.

For the expression 5x-3y+8, name the coefficient attached to x and separate the expression into its three terms.

Answer / solution
Answer: coefficient 5; terms 5x, -3y, 8

A coefficient is the numerical factor on a variable term. Terms are separated by addition/subtraction signs.

P1-1.4-INTRO-06 · Intro · 1.4-LT · 1.4-INTRO-IC2 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC2 — Substitute values into an expression.
1.4-LT — Read, evaluate, and rewrite expressions without changing their value.

Evaluate \(3x-2y+5\) for \(x=-4\) and \(y=4\).

Answer / solution
Answer: -15

Substitute with parentheses: 3(-4)-2(4)+5=-15.

P1-1.4-INTRO-07 · Intro · 1.4-LT · 1.4-INTRO-IC3 · ERROR-DIAGNOSE-WORKED-SOLUTION-01 · worked_expression
IC3 — Combine like terms.
1.4-LT — Read, evaluate, and rewrite expressions without changing their value.

A student simplifies \(3x+4+2x^2\) as \(9x^2\). Identify the error and write the correct simplified expression.

Answer / solution
Answer: 2x²+3x+4

The terms have different variable parts, so they are not like terms and cannot all be combined.

P1-1.4-INTRO-08 · Intro · 1.4-LT · 1.4-INTRO-IC4 · ERROR-DIAGNOSE-WORKED-SOLUTION-01 · worked_expression
IC4 — Use the distributive property correctly.
1.4-LT — Read, evaluate, and rewrite expressions without changing their value.

A student rewrites \(4(2x-3)\) as \(8x-3\). Identify the error and write the correct equivalent expression.

Answer / solution
Answer: 8x-12

The factor 4 must multiply both terms inside the parentheses: 4·2x=8x and 4·(-3)=-12.

P1-1.4-REVIEW-01 · Review · U2-MG02 · U2-MG02-IC1 · INVERSE-GIVEN-OUTPUT-FIND-INPUT-01 · equation_relationship
IC1 — Solve a one-step inequality.
U2-MG02 — Solve one-step inequalities and show the solution on a number line.

Solve \(x-4 \le -2\).

Answer / solution
Answer: x ≤ 2

Add 4 to both sides: x≤2.

P1-1.4-REVIEW-02 · Review · U2-MG02 · U2-MG02-IC2 · INVERSE-GIVEN-OUTPUT-FIND-INPUT-01 · equation_relationship
IC2 — Reverse the inequality sign when multiplying or dividing by a negative number.
U2-MG02 — Solve one-step inequalities and show the solution on a number line.

Solve \(-3x \ge -9\).

Answer / solution
Answer: x ≤ 3

Divide by -3. Because you divide by a negative, reverse the inequality: x≤3.

Use a open circle because the endpoint is not included, then shade left.

P1-1.4-REVIEW-04 · Review · U2-MG02 · U2-MG02-IC4 · REPRESENTATION-CONSTRUCT-OR-CRITIQUE-01 · student_constructed_number_line
IC4 — Shade the correct direction on a number line.
U2-MG02 — Solve one-step inequalities and show the solution on a number line.

Graph the solution \(x ≥ 4\) on the blank number line.

Answer / solution
Answer: closed circle at 4, shade right

Use a closed circle because the endpoint is included, then shade right.

P1-1.4-REVIEW-05 · Review · U2-MG02 · U2-MG02-IC1 · INVERSE-GIVEN-OUTPUT-FIND-INPUT-01 · equation_relationship
IC1 — Solve a one-step inequality.
U2-MG02 — Solve one-step inequalities and show the solution on a number line.

Solve \(x+3<7\), then verify with one value from your solution set.

Answer / solution
Answer: x < 4

Subtract 3 to get x<4. A test such as x=3 makes the original inequality true.

P1-1.4-REVIEW-06 · Review · U2-MG02 · U2-MG02-IC2 · ERROR-DIAGNOSE-WORKED-SOLUTION-01 · worked_expression
IC2 — Reverse the inequality sign when multiplying or dividing by a negative number.
U2-MG02 — Solve one-step inequalities and show the solution on a number line.

A student solves \(-2x<8\) as \(x<-4\). Identify the error and give the correct solution.

Answer / solution
Answer: x > -4

Dividing by -2 requires reversing the inequality sign, so x>-4.

P1-1.4-MASTERY-01 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-CONSTRUCT-OR-CRITIQUE-01 · student_constructed_coordinate_graph
IC1 — Plot and read ordered pairs.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Plot the points (-2, 6), (3, -4), (7, 1) on the coordinate plane. Then name the point with the smallest x-coordinate.

Answer / solution
Answer: (-2, 6)

The smallest x-coordinate is -2, so the point is (-2,6).

P1-1.4-MASTERY-02 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC2 · REPRESENTATION-CONSTRUCT-OR-CRITIQUE-01 · student_constructed_coordinate_graph
IC2 — Use the x- and y-axes correctly.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Plot the points (-7, 3), (4, -2), (-1, -6) on the coordinate plane. Then identify the leftmost point.

Answer / solution
Answer: (-7, 3)

The point with x=-7 is farthest left, so the answer is (-7,3).

P1-1.4-MASTERY-03 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC3 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC3 — Read a graph using its scale.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

At \(x=3\), what y-coordinate is shown by the graphed relationship?

Answer / solution
Answer: 5

At x=3, the plotted line has y=5.

P1-1.4-MASTERY-04 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC4 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC4 — Identify and interpret intercepts when they are shown.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Read the graph and report the coordinate where the line crosses the y-axis.

Answer / solution
Answer: (0, 4)

The line crosses the y-axis at y=4, so the y-intercept is (0,4).

P1-1.4-MASTERY-05 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC1 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC1 — Plot and read ordered pairs.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Read the coordinates of points B and C from the graph.

Answer / solution
Answer: B=(1, -4), C=(6, 2)

Read x first and y second from the axes: B=(1,-4) and C=(6,2).

P1-1.4-MASTERY-06 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC2 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC2 — Use the x- and y-axes correctly.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Point C is shown on the graph. What is its y-coordinate, and which axis tells you that value?

Answer / solution
Answer: -3; the y-axis

Point C is at (5,-3), so its y-coordinate is -3; y-values are read from the y-axis.

P1-1.4-MASTERY-07 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC3 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC3 — Read a graph using its scale.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Use the graph and its scale to find the y-value when \(x=1\).

Answer / solution
Answer: 3

At x=1, the plotted line has y=3.

P1-1.4-MASTERY-08 · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC4 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_context_graph
IC4 — Identify and interpret intercepts when they are shown.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Locate the graph's y-intercept and explain what the y-value means before any time has passed.

Answer / solution
Answer: (0, 7); the amount starts at 7 units

The graph crosses the y-axis at (0,7), so the starting amount is 7 units.

Section 1.5 · Practice 1

Intro · 8 questions

P1-1.5-INTRO-01 · Intro · 1.5-LT · 1.5-INTRO-IC1 · REPTRANS-TRANSLATE-VERBAL-SYMBOLIC-01 · context_to_symbolic
IC1 — Define a variable.
1.5-LT — Write an equation from a situation and explain what its solution means.

A club charges $9 to join plus $3 per event. Define a variable for the number of events attended.

\( ext{variable}= ext{meaning}\)
Answer / solution
Answer: Let e = number of events attended.

A useful variable names the unknown quantity and its meaning: e is the number of events attended.

P1-1.5-INTRO-02 · Intro · 1.5-LT · 1.5-INTRO-IC2 · REPTRANS-TRANSLATE-VERBAL-SYMBOLIC-01 · context_to_equation
IC2 — Write an equation that matches a situation.
1.5-LT — Write an equation from a situation and explain what its solution means.

A club charges $10 to join plus $4 per event. A member paid $30. Let \(e\) be the number of events. Write an equation that matches the situation.

Answer / solution
Answer: 10+4e=30

Starting fee + rate·events = total, so 10+4e=30.

P1-1.5-INTRO-03 · Intro · 1.5-LT · 1.5-INTRO-IC3 · INVERSE-GIVEN-OUTPUT-FIND-INPUT-01 · equation_relationship
IC3 — Use equivalent steps to solve when needed.
1.5-LT — Write an equation from a situation and explain what its solution means.

Solve \(11+5e=41\) for \(e\).

Answer / solution
Answer: 6

Subtract 11, then divide by 5. This gives e=6.

P1-1.5-INTRO-04 · Intro · 1.5-LT · 1.5-INTRO-IC4 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · context_equation
IC4 — Check and interpret a solution in context.
1.5-LT — Write an equation from a situation and explain what its solution means.

A club charges $12 plus $6 per event. The equation \(12+6e=36\) models a member's total. Solve, check by substitution, and interpret \(e\).

Answer / solution
Answer: e=4; 4 events

Solve to get e=4. Check: 12+6(4)=36. Therefore the member attended 4 events.

P1-1.5-INTRO-05 · Intro · 1.5-LT · 1.5-INTRO-IC1 · REPTRANS-TRANSLATE-VERBAL-SYMBOLIC-01 · context_to_symbolic
IC1 — Define a variable.
1.5-LT — Write an equation from a situation and explain what its solution means.

A club charges $8 to join plus $3 per event. Define a variable for the number of events attended.

\( ext{variable}= ext{meaning}\)
Answer / solution
Answer: Let e = number of events attended.

A useful variable names the unknown quantity and its meaning: e is the number of events attended.

P1-1.5-INTRO-06 · Intro · 1.5-LT · 1.5-INTRO-IC2 · REPTRANS-TRANSLATE-VERBAL-SYMBOLIC-01 · context_to_equation
IC2 — Write an equation that matches a situation.
1.5-LT — Write an equation from a situation and explain what its solution means.

A club charges $9 to join plus $4 per event. A member paid $33. Let \(e\) be the number of events. Write an equation that matches the situation.

Answer / solution
Answer: 9+4e=33

Starting fee + rate·events = total, so 9+4e=33.

P1-1.5-INTRO-08 · Intro · 1.5-LT · 1.5-INTRO-IC4 · ERROR-CRITIQUE-CLAIM-01 · context_equation
IC4 — Check and interpret a solution in context.
1.5-LT — Write an equation from a situation and explain what its solution means.

For \(11+6e=41\), a student says: “The solution e=5 means the member paid $5.” Evaluate and correct the interpretation.

Answer / solution
Answer: e=5 means 5 events

The variable e was defined as the number of events, so the solution means the member attended 5 events, not that the payment was $5.

P1-1.5-REVIEW-01 · Review · U2-MG01 · U2-MG01-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC1 — Compare two relationships shown in different representations.
U2-MG01 — Compare relationships across graphs, tables, equations, and contexts using clear evidence.

Compare Relationships A and B using both rate of change and starting value. Which one starts higher, and which one grows faster?

xy (B)
04
210
416
Answer / solution
Answer: A rate=2, start=5; B rate=3, start=4; A starts higher; B grows faster

Compare the same numerical features in both representations: calculate each rate of change and identify each starting value before making the claim.

P1-1.5-REVIEW-05 · Review · U2-MG01 · U2-MG01-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_table_equation
IC1 — Compare two relationships shown in different representations.
U2-MG01 — Compare relationships across graphs, tables, equations, and contexts using clear evidence.

Compare the rate of change in the table with the rate in \(y=-x+3\). Include the sign of each rate and describe what it says about growth or decrease.

xy (A)
06
210
414
Answer / solution
Answer: A rate=2 and is increasing; B rate=-1 and is decreasing

From the table, A has rate 2 and is increasing. In \(y=-x+3\), B has rate -1 and is decreasing.

P1-1.5-REVIEW-07 · Review · U2-MG01 · U2-MG01-IC3 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_table_equation
IC3 — Explain which relationship is greater, faster, or starts higher when the question asks.
U2-MG01 — Compare relationships across graphs, tables, equations, and contexts using clear evidence.

One relationship starts higher while the other grows faster. Compare the table with \(y=x+5\) and show the starting values and rates that support your claim.

xy (A)
0-2
24
410
Answer / solution
Answer: A rate=3, start=-2; B rate=1, start=5; B starts higher; A grows faster

Compare the same numerical features in both representations: calculate each rate of change and identify each starting value before making the claim.

P1-1.5-MASTERY-01 · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC1 · REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01 · semantic_data_table
IC1 — Use input/output language and ordered pairs.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

Use the table. Write the ordered pair for the row with input \(3\), using \((input, output)\) order.

input xoutput y
-16
12
3-2
5-6
Answer / solution
Answer: (3, -2)

Ordered pairs are written (input, output), so the pair is (3, -2).

P1-1.5-MASTERY-02 · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC2 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC2 — Use a rule to find an output from an input.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

Use the rule \(y=4x+0\) to find the output for input 4. Record the ordered pair.

Answer / solution
Answer: (4, 16)

Substitute each input into the rule. The resulting ordered pair(s) are (4, 16).

P1-1.5-MASTERY-03 · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC3 · INVERSE-GIVEN-OUTPUT-FIND-INPUT-01 · equation_relationship
IC3 — Find a missing input or output when enough information is given.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

The rule is \(y=-3x+5\). If the output is -13, find the input.

Answer / solution
Answer: 6

Set -13=-3x+5 and undo the operations. The input is x=6.

P1-1.5-MASTERY-04 · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC4 · REPRESENTATION-CONSTRUCT-OR-CRITIQUE-01 · student_constructed_table
IC4 — Use a rule to build or extend a table.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

input xoutput y
-1-5
1 
37
6 
Answer / solution

Apply y=3x-2 to each missing row. The missing outputs are 1 and 16.

P1-1.5-MASTERY-05 · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC1 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · semantic_data_table
IC1 — Use input/output language and ordered pairs.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

In the table, locate the row where the input is 3 and report the ordered pair in input-output order.

input xoutput y
-1-5
11
37
513
Answer / solution
Answer: (3, 7)

Ordered pairs are written (input, output), so the pair is (3, 7).

P1-1.5-MASTERY-06 · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC2 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC2 — Use a rule to find an output from an input.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

Use the rule \(y=-2x+4\) to find the outputs for inputs [-2, 1, 4].

Answer / solution
Answer: (-2, 8), (1, 2), (4, -4)

Substitute each input into the rule. The resulting ordered pair(s) are (-2, 8), (1, 2), (4, -4).

P1-1.5-MASTERY-07 · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC3 · INVERSE-GIVEN-OUTPUT-FIND-INPUT-01 · equation_relationship
IC3 — Find a missing input or output when enough information is given.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

The rule is \(y=4x+0\). If the output is 20, find the input.

Answer / solution
Answer: 5

Set 20=4x+0 and undo the operations. The input is x=5.

P1-1.5-MASTERY-08 · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC4 · PATTERN-EXTEND-NUMERIC-01 · semantic_data_table
IC4 — Use a rule to build or extend a table.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

Use the pattern in the table to state the rule and predict the output when \(x=7\).

input xoutput y
-18
12
3-4
5-10
Answer / solution
Answer: y=-3x+5; -16

The outputs change consistently with y=-3x+5. Substituting x=7 gives -16.

Section 1.1 · Official Exit Ticket

EXIT-1.1-Q1-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-CONSTRUCT-OR-CRITIQUE-01 · student_constructed_coordinate_graph
IC1 — Plot and read ordered pairs.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Plot \((-3,3)\), \((2,-3)\), \((5,-1)\). Then write the coordinates of the point farthest left.

Answer / solution
Answer: (-3, 3)

Plot each ordered pair using x first and y second. The farthest-left point has the smallest x-coordinate, so it is (-3, 3).

EXIT-1.1-Q3-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC2 · REPRESENTATION-CONSTRUCT-OR-CRITIQUE-01 · student_constructed_coordinate_graph
IC2 — Use the x- and y-axes correctly.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Plot \((-5,4)\), \((3,-2)\), \((-2,-5)\). Then write the coordinates of the point farthest left.

Answer / solution
Answer: (-5, 4)

Plot each ordered pair using x first and y second. The farthest-left point has the smallest x-coordinate, so it is (-5, 4).

EXIT-1.1-Q4-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC4 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_context_graph
IC4 — Identify and interpret intercepts when they are shown.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Use the graph. Identify the y-intercept and explain what it represents as the starting amount.

Answer / solution
Answer: (0, 8); the amount starts at 8 units

At x=0 the graph is at y=8. The y-intercept is (0, 8), so the starting amount is 8 units.

EXIT-1.2-Q1-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG03 · U1-MG03-IC1 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC1 — Substitute a value for a variable.
U1-MG03 — Evaluate numerical and algebraic expressions using substitution and order of operations.

Evaluate \(5x+3\) for \(x=5\).

Answer / solution
Answer: 28

Substitute the given value(s) with parentheses and follow order of operations. The result is 28.

EXIT-1.2-Q2-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG03 · U1-MG03-IC3 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · equation_relationship
IC3 — Evaluate expressions with positive and negative numbers.
U1-MG03 — Evaluate numerical and algebraic expressions using substitution and order of operations.

For \(x=-7\), a student claims \(2x+6=8\). Evaluate the expression and decide whether the claim is reasonable.

Answer / solution
Answer: -8; claim is not reasonable

Substitute x=-7: 2(-7)+6=-8. Because the product is negative with magnitude greater than 6, a positive result is not reasonable.

Section 1.3 · Official Exit Ticket

EXIT-1.3-Q1-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC1 — Find slope from a graph.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

Find the rate of change from the graph and from the table. Do they show the same rate?

xy
01
25
511
Answer / solution
Answer: 2; yes

Both representations change by 2 units in y for each 1 unit in x, so they have the same rate.

EXIT-1.3-Q2-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC3 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · context_quantitative
IC3 — Find slope from an equation.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

A machine produces \(12\) parts by hour \(2\) and \(24\) parts by hour \(6\), at a constant rate. Find and interpret the rate.

Answer / solution
Answer: 3 parts/hour

Rate=(24-12)/(6-2)=3. Production increases by 3 parts for each additional hour.

EXIT-1.3-Q3-REASSESS · Mastery · U1-MG03 · U1-MG03-IC1 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC1 — Substitute a value for a variable.
U1-MG03 — Evaluate numerical and algebraic expressions using substitution and order of operations.

Evaluate \(4x - 2y + 5\) for \(x=0\), \(y=5\).

Answer / solution
Answer: -5

Substitute the given value(s) with parentheses and follow order of operations. The result is -5.

EXIT-1.3-Q4-REASSESS · Mastery · U1-MG03 · U1-MG03-IC3 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · equation_relationship
IC3 — Evaluate expressions with positive and negative numbers.
U1-MG03 — Evaluate numerical and algebraic expressions using substitution and order of operations.

For \(x=-5\), a student claims \(4x+5=15\). Evaluate the expression and decide whether the claim is reasonable.

Answer / solution
Answer: -15; claim is not reasonable

Substitute x=-5: 4(-5)+5=-15. Because the product is negative with magnitude greater than 5, a positive result is not reasonable.

EXIT-1.4-Q2-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC4 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_context_graph
IC4 — Identify and interpret intercepts when they are shown.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Use the graph. Identify the y-intercept and explain what it represents as the starting amount.

Answer / solution
Answer: (0, 6); the amount starts at 6 units

At x=0 the graph is at y=6. The y-intercept is (0, 6), so the starting amount is 6 units.

EXIT-1.4-Q3-REASSESS · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC1 · REPRESENTATION-READ-THEN-CALCULATE-01 · mixed_graph_table
IC1 — Find slope from a graph.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

Calculate the rate of change shown by the graph and by the table. Do the two representations agree?

xy
05
21
5-5
Answer / solution
Answer: -2; yes

Both representations change by -2 units in y for each 1 unit in x, so they have the same rate.

EXIT-1.4-Q4-REASSESS · Mastery · U1-MG02 · U1-MG02-IC3 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · context_quantitative
IC3 — Find slope from an equation.
U1-MG02 — Find slope or rate of change from a graph, table, equation, or context.

A tank has \(16\) liters after \(2\) minutes and \(32\) liters after \(6\) minutes, changing at a constant rate. Find the rate and predict the amount after \(7\) minutes.

Answer / solution
Answer: 4 L/min; 36 L

Rate=(32-16)/(6-2)=4 L/min. Extending the linear relationship gives 36 L at 7 minutes.

Section 1.5 · Official Exit Ticket

EXIT-1.5-Q1-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC2 · FORMULA-DIRECT-RELATIONSHIP-01 · equation_relationship
IC2 — Use a rule to find an output from an input.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

Use the rule \(y=2x+1\) to find the output for input 5. Record the ordered pair.

Answer / solution
Answer: (5, 11)

Substitute each input into the rule. The resulting ordered pair(s) are (5, 11).

EXIT-1.5-Q2-NEW · Mastery · U1-MG04 · U1-MG04-IC3 · REPRESENTATION-CONSTRUCT-OR-CRITIQUE-01 · student_constructed_table
IC3 — Find a missing input or output when enough information is given.
U1-MG04 — Use an input-output rule to find values, ordered pairs, and patterns.

Use the rule \(y=2x+1\). Complete the missing output, then extend the table to \(x=7\).

input xoutput y
-1-1
1 
37
7 
Answer / solution
Answer: at x=1, y=3; at x=7, y=15

Apply the rule to each missing row. The missing values are 3 and 15.

EXIT-1.5-Q3-REASSESS · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC3 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_coordinate_graph
IC3 — Read a graph using its scale.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Use the graph and its scale to find the y-value when \(x=-4\).

Answer / solution
Answer: -2

At x=-4, the point on the line has y=-2.

EXIT-1.5-Q4-REASSESS · Mastery · U1-MG01 · U1-MG01-IC4 · FEATURE-IDENTIFY-FROM-REPRESENTATION-01 · supplied_context_graph
IC4 — Identify and interpret intercepts when they are shown.
U1-MG01 — Use ordered pairs, axes, scales, and intercepts to read and create graphs.

Use the graph. Identify the y-intercept and explain what it represents as the starting amount.

Answer / solution
Answer: (0, 10); the amount starts at 10 units

At x=0 the graph is at y=10. The y-intercept is (0, 10), so the starting amount is 10 units.

Part (d)

Section 1.4 · WTC Routine

WTC-1.4 · Intro · 1.4-LT · 1.4-INTRO-IC1 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · equation_relationship
IC1 — Identify terms, coefficients, and factors.
1.4-LT — Read, evaluate, and rewrite expressions without changing their value.

Use the shared expression for every part.

\(3(2x-5)+4x\)

Part (a)

Identify the factor that controls the first rewrite and the terms inside its parentheses.

Part (b)

Apply the distributive property to rewrite the expression.

Part (c)

Use your result from part (b) to combine like terms.

Part (d)

Check the original and simplified forms at x=2 and explain what the matching values show.

Section 1.5 · WTC Routine

WTC-1.5 · Intro · 1.5-LT · 1.5-INTRO-IC1 · PROC-MULTISTEP-CHAIN-01 · context_equation
IC1 — Define a variable.
1.5-LT — Write an equation from a situation and explain what its solution means.

A bike rental costs $12 plus $4 per hour. A customer pays $32 total.

\(\text{Equation: }\_\_\_\_\)

Part (a)

Define a variable for the number of rental hours.

Part (b)

Use your variable from part (a) to write an equation for the total cost.

Part (c)

Solve your equation from part (b) and check the solution in the original equation.

Part (d)

Interpret the checked solution from part (c) in the situation.
